I just… figured I should talk to someone, if that’s alright.\n\nDoctor: Did you think about climbing on the tractor before you actually did it?\n\nPatient: Not really in a sensible way, no. I mean, I was up there giving my talk and then I just… got it in my head I needed to show them that “new pattern” right then. So I climbed on and started moving the controls. I know that sounds bad, Doctor, and I wasn’t trying to scare anybody. People were yelling for me to stop, and I heard them, but I just couldn’t make myself quit at that moment.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like you couldn't control yourself when you climbed onto the tractor?\n\nPatient: Yes, Doctor… I think that’s fair to say. I don’t like putting it that way, because it makes me sound reckless, but once I got up there and started jerking those controls around to show that “new pattern,” I couldn’t seem to stop myself. Even with folks yelling at me, I just kept going for a bit.\n\nDoctor: Has anything like this happened to you before?\n\nPatient: Well… not quite like that, no. I mean, I’ve gotten carried away before, I suppose, but the thing they brought me in for was that fair business—me climbing up on the tractor during my talk and jerking the controls around to show that “new pattern.” Folks were yelling, and I knew it wasn’t right, but I just couldn’t seem to stop myself right then. I know that doesn’t sound good, Doctor.\n\nDoctor: Have you often found yourself doing things on impulse that you later regret?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say “often,” exactly… I don’t want to make it sound like I’m always doing things like that. But yes, I did regret what happened at the fair. I got up on that tractor during my talk and kept jerking the controls to show that “new pattern,” and even with people yelling at me to stop, I just… couldn’t seem to make myself stop right then. I know that wasn’t okay.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually act on impulse without thinking through the consequences?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I usually do, Doctor… at least I hope not. I try to think things through. But with the fair, yes, I guess that was impulsive. I climbed up on that tractor during my talk and started working the controls to show everyone that “new pattern,” and even when people were yelling at me, I couldn’t make myself stop right away. That part worries me, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Have you consistently acted on impulse throughout your life?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, Doctor. At least… not consistently, no. I mean, I’ve made mistakes like anyone, but I wouldn’t say my whole life has been me just acting without thinking. If there’s something in particular you’re looking for, I can try to answer it better.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty getting along with others due to your behavior or attitudes?\n\nPatient: I try to get along with people, Doctor. I don’t want to be difficult. But… yes, lately my behavior has made things harder with my wife and the workers.\n\nThey’ve had to step in and take over decisions I used to handle myself—things with lenders, equipment use, even the day-to-day running of the farm—because people don’t feel it’s safe with me in charge right now. And after what happened at the fair, I think folks started wondering if I could keep running the farm at all.\n\nThat’s hard to say out loud, but I know it’s true.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been in serious trouble with the law?\n\nPatient: No, Doctor… not serious trouble, no. I’ve tried to stay on the right side of things. People were hollering for me to stop, and I knew it wasn’t going over right, but I just couldn’t seem to make myself quit right then. I’m not trying to cause trouble, Doctor. I just… figured I should talk to someone, if that’s alright.\n\nDoctor: Did you think about climbing on the tractor before you actually did it?\n\nPatient: Not really in a sensible way, no. I mean, I was up there giving my talk and then I just… got it in my head I needed to show them that “new pattern” right then. So I climbed on and started moving the controls. I know that sounds bad, Doctor, and I wasn’t trying to scare anybody. People were yelling for me to stop, and I heard them, but I just couldn’t make myself quit at that moment.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like you couldn't control yourself when you climbed onto the tractor?\n\nPatient: Yes, Doctor… I think that’s fair to say. I don’t like putting it that way, because it makes me sound reckless, but once I got up there and started jerking those controls around to show that “new pattern,” I couldn’t seem to stop myself. Even with folks yelling at me, I just kept going for a bit.\n\nDoctor: Has anything like this happened to you before?\n\nPatient: Well… not quite like that, no. I mean, I’ve gotten carried away before, I suppose, but the thing they brought me in for was that fair business—me climbing up on the tractor during my talk and jerking the controls around to show that “new pattern.” Folks were yelling, and I knew it wasn’t right, but I just couldn’t seem to stop myself right then. I know that doesn’t sound good, Doctor.\n\nDoctor: Have you often found yourself doing things on impulse that you later regret?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say “often,” exactly… I don’t want to make it sound like I’m always doing things like that. But yes, I did regret what happened at the fair. I got up on that tractor during my talk and kept jerking the controls to show that “new pattern,” and even with people yelling at me to stop, I just… couldn’t seem to make myself stop right then. I know that wasn’t okay.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually act on impulse without thinking through the consequences?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I usually do, Doctor… at least I hope not. I try to think things through. But with the fair, yes, I guess that was impulsive. I climbed up on that tractor during my talk and started working the controls to show everyone that “new pattern,” and even when people were yelling at me, I couldn’t make myself stop right away. That part worries me, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Have you consistently acted on impulse throughout your life?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, Doctor. At least… not consistently, no. I mean, I’ve made mistakes like anyone, but I wouldn’t say my whole life has been me just acting without thinking. If there’s something in particular you’re looking for, I can try to answer it better.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty getting along with others due to your behavior or attitudes?\n\nPatient: I try to get along with people, Doctor. I don’t want to be difficult. But… yes, lately my behavior has made things harder with my wife and the workers.\n\nThey’ve had to step in and take over decisions I used to handle myself—things with lenders, equipment use, even the day-to-day running of the farm—because people don’t feel it’s safe with me in charge right now. And after what happened at the fair, I think folks started wondering if I could keep running the farm at all.\n\nThat’s hard to say out loud, but I know it’s true.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been in serious trouble with the law?\n\nPatient: No, Doctor… not serious trouble, no. I’ve tried to stay on the right side of things. I mean, I’m not saying I’ve been perfect in life, but nothing like being arrested or charged with anything serious that I can think of.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others or finish their sentences for them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I do that often, Doctor. I might jump in now and then if I’m nervous or trying to explain myself, but I don’t believe I’m usually the kind of person who cuts people off or finishes their sentences. Is that the kind of outburst you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you consistently shown a lack of concern for how your actions affect others throughout your life?\n\nPatient: No, Doctor, I don’t think so… not consistently, anyhow. I usually do worry about whether I’ve hurt someone or made things harder. If anything, I’d like to think I’ve tried to do right by people. Does that answer it the way you mean?\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you acted impulsively like this for most of your life?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 60 / farmer_rancher_or_agricultural_manager / high_school\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had outbursts of anger where you lost control?\n\nPatient: Well… yes, I suppose I have, though I don’t like to think of it as “losing control” exactly. Some mornings I wake up feeling almost too good, like the whole valley’s opening up for me, and I’m just grinning at nothing. But then if a hired hand questions what I’m doing, or my wife tells me I ought to slow down, I can snap pretty hard… like they’ve insulted the family name or something. I’m not proud of that. I do try to catch myself, Doctor. Is that the kind of outburst you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you consistently shown a lack of concern for how your actions affect others throughout your life?\n\nPatient: No, Doctor, I don’t think so… not consistently, anyhow. I’ve made mistakes, sure, and I can be stubborn, but I wouldn’t say I’ve gone through life not caring how people are affected by me. I usually do worry about whether I’ve hurt someone or made things harder. If anything, I’d like to think I’ve tried to do right by people. Does that answer it the way you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you acted impulsively like this for most of your life?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say “most of my life,” Doctor. I mean… I’ve had times where I acted quicker than I should’ve, sure, but I don’t know that I’ve been that way all along. I’ve usually tried to be responsible and think things through, especially with work and family. Maybe there are moments I’m not seeing clearly, but I wouldn’t want to make it sound worse than it is. Is that answering what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Would you say acting on impulse is something that has been a part of your behavior since you were young?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think so, Doctor. I mean, when I was young I could be headstrong like any boy, I suppose, but I wouldn’t say acting on impulse was some lifelong pattern with me. I’ve generally tried to be steady and do what was expected. If there are things you’re hearing that make it sound otherwise, I can try to think harder on it.\n\nDoctor: Has acting on impulse without thinking been a common way for you to behave for as long as you can remember?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think that’s been common for me, Doctor. I’ve had moments, sure, like anybody, where I spoke too fast or made a quick choice, but I wouldn’t say that’s been how I’ve been for as long as I can remember. I’ve mostly tried to be careful and think things through. If I’m missing what you mean by “acting on impulse,” you can tell me.\n\nDoctor: Has acting on impulse been a part of your behavior since childhood?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say it’s been there since childhood, no. I’m sure I had times as a kid where I did something quick without thinking, but nothing that I’d call a pattern. I don’t want to overstate it. I was mostly just trying to do what I was supposed to do. Is that what you mean?"
